Figure 9.
The small contour
\displaystyle C_{a}
, which avoids the shifted cut
\displaystyle L_{1}

Let us compute asymptotics of the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(2i\pi)
on the subcontours
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
,
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
.

On the subarc
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
:
we can use the fact that
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+}(z))<\Im(f_{+}(\upsilon^{\prime\prime}))=0
for all
\displaystyle z
in the interior of
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
. Let us decompose
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
into consecutive subarcs
\displaystyle C_{a}^{--}
,
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-+}
with initial endpoint
\displaystyle-i\eta
and final endpoint
\displaystyle-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
respectively, such that along
\displaystyle C_{a}^{--}
we have
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+})<-c
for some
\displaystyle c>0
. Then:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\int_{C_{a}^{--}}g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi)}dz\right|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\max_{C_{a}^{--}}(|g_{N}(z)|)\int_{C_{a}^{--}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}|dz|

(139)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\max_{C_{a}^{--}}(|g_{N}(z)|)\int_{C_{a}^{--}}e^{-\frac{N}{2\pi}c}|dz|=o_{N\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{{N}}\right).

Here we use that
\displaystyle\textstyle|g_{N}(z)|
is bounded from above by a constant independent of
\displaystyle N
on
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
. Indeed
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
is compact and stays away from a fixed small disc centered at
\displaystyle 0
, so (
120
) implies that
\displaystyle|\rho(z)|
is bounded from above by a constant independent of
\displaystyle N
on
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
. The formula of
\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}_{u_{0},N}(z)}
above (
128
) and Lemma
3.5
imply this as well for
\displaystyle\textstyle|\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}{\mathcal{I}_{u_{0},N}(z)}\right)|
and
\displaystyle\textstyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}{\Psi_{u_{0},N}(z)}\right)
, respectively, and clearly it holds for the term
\displaystyle\textstyle\left(1+\frac{1}{N}O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\left(1\right)\right)\coth\left(\frac{Nz}{2}\right)
in (
130
). Hence
\displaystyle\textstyle g_{N}(z)=\rho(z)\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}{\Psi_{u_{0},N}(z)}\right)\textstyle(1+\frac{1}{N}R_{N}(z))
has modulus bounded on
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
by a constant independent of
\displaystyle N
.

Along the subarc
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-+}
we can apply Proposition
5.3
(ii) with
\displaystyle n=N/2\pi
,
\displaystyle f=f_{+}
, and
\displaystyle(g_{n})
the sequence
\displaystyle(g_{N})
; indeed,
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-+}
is a compact subset of
\displaystyle U
, so Lemma
3.5
(iii) and the bounds discussed above for
\displaystyle R_{N}(z)
imply that
\displaystyle(g_{N})
converges uniformly to
\displaystyle\rho(z)
on
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-+}
. We have
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+}(\upsilon^{\prime\prime}))=0
,
\displaystyle f_{+}^{\prime}(\upsilon^{\prime\prime})\neq 0
, and
\displaystyle\rho(\upsilon^{\prime\prime})\neq 0
, so from (
137
) we deduce

\left|\int_{C_{a}^{-+}}g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi)}dz\right|\sim_{N\to+\infty}D\cdot\frac{1}{N}

for a scalar
\displaystyle D\neq 0
(we will not use its actual value).

Consider now the subarc
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
.
Unlike in the case of
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
above, the function
\displaystyle g_{N}
is not bounded on
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
since its factor
\displaystyle\rho(z)
satisfies
\displaystyle|\rho(z)|\to+\infty
as
\displaystyle z:=is_{N}^{-}\to 0
. Let us see precisely what happens.

Part of the above discussion about
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
is still true. Namely, by Lemma
3.5
(ii) the function
\displaystyle\textstyle|\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}{\Psi_{u_{0},N}}(z)\right)|
is bounded from above by a constant independent of
\displaystyle N
and
\displaystyle z\in C_{a^{+}}
. Also, by the comment after (
129
) the same is true for obvious reasons for the modulus of the factor
\displaystyle\textstyle\left(1+\frac{1}{N}O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\left(1\right)\right)\coth(Nz/2)
in (
135
), since
\displaystyle\coth(Nis_{N}^{-}/2)=\coth(i\alpha^{-}\pi/2)
.

Consider
\displaystyle\rho(z)
. We claim that, because
\displaystyle z=is_{N}^{-}
is the point of
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
which is the closest to the singularity
\displaystyle z=0
of
\displaystyle\rho(z)
, it is enough to consider the behaviour of
\displaystyle\rho(is_{N}^{-})
. Indeed, we can deform
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
slightly upwards near
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
, so that it reaches a point
\displaystyle i\eta
with
\displaystyle s_{N}^{-}<\eta<\pi
, and then goes vertically to
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
. By the same arguments as for
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
,
\displaystyle|\rho(z)|
is bounded from above by a constant independent of
\displaystyle N
on the subarc from
\displaystyle-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
to
\displaystyle i\eta
. For
\displaystyle z=it
on the vertical subarc from
\displaystyle i\eta
to
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
, it is readily checked from the formula (
120
) that

|\rho(it)|={\rm(Constant})\cdot\exp\left(-t\left(\frac{{\rm Log}|u_{1}|}{2\pi}+\frac{{\rm Log}|u_{0}|}{4\pi}\right)-\frac{\mathfrak{I}({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime})+\pi}{2\pi}\cdot\ln(\sin(t/2))\right).

Our choice of
\displaystyle a_{0}\geq 2
makes
\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+\pi)/2\pi=\mathfrak{R}(({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi)>0
, and the derivative of
\displaystyle|\rho(it)|
is

\left(-\left(\frac{{\rm Log}|u_{1}|}{2\pi}+\frac{{\rm Log}|u_{0}|}{4\pi}\right)-\frac{\mathfrak{I}({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime})+\pi}{2\pi}{\rm cotan}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)\right)\cdot|\rho(it)|,

which is negative for
\displaystyle t>0
and small, as
\displaystyle\textstyle\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}{\rm cotan}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)=+\infty
. The maximum of
\displaystyle|\rho(it)|
is therefore attained at
\displaystyle|\rho(is_{N}^{-})|
on the subarc from
\displaystyle-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
to
\displaystyle i\eta
. This concludes the proof of our claim.

Now we have
\displaystyle 1-e^{z}=\textstyle\frac{1}{N}O(1)\neq 0
for every
\displaystyle\textstyle z\sim_{N\to+\infty}\frac{u}{N}
,
\displaystyle u\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
, so the formula (
120
) implies that
\displaystyle N^{-({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}|\rho(is_{N}^{-})|
has modulus bounded from above by a constant independent of
\displaystyle N
(note again that our choice of
\displaystyle a_{0}\geq 2
makes
\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}(({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi)>0
, whence
\displaystyle N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}\to+\infty
as
\displaystyle N\to+\infty
). A similar argument applied to the integral formula of
\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}_{u_{0},N}(z)}
shows that the same conclusion holds true for
\displaystyle\textstyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}{\mathcal{I}_{u_{0},N}(is_{N}^{-})}\right)
.

Summing up, we obtain that
\displaystyle N^{-({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}g_{N}
is bounded from above by a constant independent of
\displaystyle N
on
\displaystyle C_{a^{+}}
. Then let us write

(140)

\int_{C_{a}^{+}}g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi)}dz=N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}\int_{C_{a}^{+}}\frac{g_{N}(z)}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}dz.

Now, from Section
4.5.1
we have

\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\Im(f_{+}(x+it))=2t-\arg\left(1-e^{x+it}\right).

Since
\displaystyle\arg\left(1-e^{x+it}\right)<0
when
\displaystyle x<0
and
\displaystyle t>0
is close to
\displaystyle 0
, it follows that, if one initially chooses
\displaystyle\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
small enough (which, by the definition of
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
and
\displaystyle C_{a}^{-}
is always possible by letting
\displaystyle N
be large enough), then
\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\Im(f_{+}(z))>0
for any
\displaystyle z\in C_{a}^{+}
, and thus for any such
\displaystyle z=x+it
we have

(141)

e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}\leq e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(it))}=e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}{\rm Cl}_{2}(t)}.

Then, a simple upper bound of the modulus of the RHS integral in (
140
) is

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{C_{a}^{+}}\left(\frac{|g_{N}(z)|}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}\right)\int_{C_{a}^{+}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}dz\

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq\max_{C_{a}^{+}}\left(\frac{|g_{N}(z)|}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}\right)\cdot O_{N\to\infty}(1)\cdot\int_{0}^{s_{N}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}{\rm Cl}_{2}(t)}dt

(142)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=O_{N\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-\frac{\alpha^{-}}{2}}}\right)=o_{N\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),

where we used (
141
) in the inequality, and the asymptotics of the last integral computed in Section
4.3.2
in the equality. Note that the factor
\displaystyle O_{N\to\infty}(1)
on the RHS of the inequality comes from the change of measure from the (compact) contour
\displaystyle C^{+}_{a}
to the vertical contour
\displaystyle[0,is_{N}]
.

This estimate is not enough for our purposes, because it only implies that the integral (
140
) has order
\displaystyle\leq\textstyle N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}o_{N\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)
, whereas the integral over the subpath
\displaystyle C_{3}^{\prime}\cup C_{4}^{\prime}
(going from
\displaystyle a+2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}
to
\displaystyle-i\eta
, see Figure
8
) will have order exactly
\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
as in the classical case of Section
4.5.1
(see Step 5). There could be cancellations between these two integrals (the ones over the other subpaths in Figure
8
will have asymptotics of order
\displaystyle\textstyle o_{\to\infty}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}})
), and therefore we have to compute more precisely the order of the integral on the RHS of (
140
). We do this by using Proposition
5.3
.

Let us deform the arc
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
slightly downwards near its endpoint
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
, so that it reaches the axis
\displaystyle i\mathbb{R}
in a point
\displaystyle is_{N}^{\prime}
, where
\displaystyle s_{N}^{\prime}:=\textstyle\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{N}<s_{N}^{-}
, and then goes vertically upwards to
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
. Denote by
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+-}
the subarc from
\displaystyle-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
to
\displaystyle is_{N}^{\prime}
, and write

(143)

\int_{C_{a}^{+}}\frac{g_{N}(z)}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}dz=\int_{C_{a}^{+-}}\frac{g_{N}(z)}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}dz\\
+\int_{[is_{N}^{\prime},is_{N}^{-}]}\frac{g_{N}(z)}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}dz.

Instead of the integral over
\displaystyle[is_{N}^{\prime},is_{N}^{-}]
on the RHS, let us consider for a while the same integral but with the contour
\displaystyle[i\eta_{1},i\eta_{2}]
, with
\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{\pi}{3}>\eta_{2}>\eta_{1}>0
fixed. We know that for
\displaystyle z=it
, we have
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+}(z)):=\Im(\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi))=\Im({\rm Li}_{2}(e^{it})-t^{2})={\rm Cl}_{2}(t)
, and the function
\displaystyle{\rm Cl}_{2}(t)
is strictly increasing and
\displaystyle{\rm Cl}_{2}^{\prime}(t)=-{\rm Log}|2\sin(t/2)|\neq 0
for
\displaystyle t\in\textstyle]0,\frac{\pi}{3}[
. In particular
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+}(z))<\Im(f_{+}(i\eta_{2}))
for every
\displaystyle z\in[i\eta_{1},i\eta_{2}]
. All the other assumptions of Proposition
5.3
(ii) are clearly satisfied (the uniform convergence of
\displaystyle(g_{N})
to
\displaystyle\rho
on
\displaystyle[i\eta_{1},i\eta_{2}]
follows from Lemma
3.5
and the properties of
\displaystyle R_{N}
discussed in Lemma
5.1
), so this result implies (the sign in front of the RHS is because
\displaystyle[i\eta_{1},i\eta_{2}]

ends
at the maximum
\displaystyle i\eta_{2}
of
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+}(i\eta_{2}))
):

(144)

\int_{[i\eta_{1},i\eta_{2}]}\frac{g_{N}(z)}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}dz\\
\sim_{N\to+\infty}-\dfrac{e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}({\rm Li}_{2}(e^{i\eta_{2}})-\eta_{2}^{2})}}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}\dfrac{\rho(i\eta_{2})}{-i{\rm Log}|2\sin(\eta_{2}/2)|}\cdot\dfrac{1}{N}.

Now we come back to
\displaystyle\eta_{2}:=s_{N}^{-}=\textstyle\alpha^{-}\frac{\pi}{N}
,
\displaystyle\eta_{1}:=s_{N}^{\prime}=\textstyle\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{N}<s_{N}^{-}
. By Proposition
5.3
(iii) the equivalent (
144
) is uniform in
\displaystyle i\eta_{2}
as long as it stays a maximum of
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+})
along the contour, it varies in a compact set not containing a critical point of
\displaystyle f_{+}
, and
\displaystyle(g_{N})
converges uniformly to
\displaystyle\rho
on this compact set. As noted above, the first two conditions are satisfied here. As for the convergence of
\displaystyle(g_{N})
,
\displaystyle i\eta_{2}=is_{N}^{-}
leaves any compact subset of
\displaystyle\textstyle]0,i\frac{\pi}{3}[
as
\displaystyle N\to+\infty
, but the bounds (
65
) and (
128
) (with
\displaystyle\delta:=\alpha\textstyle\frac{\pi}{N}
) on any strip
\displaystyle\textstyle(\alpha+1)\frac{\pi}{N}<\mathfrak{I}(z)<2\pi-(\alpha+1)\frac{\pi}{N}
,
\displaystyle\alpha>0
, imply that
\displaystyle g_{N}(\textstyle\alpha^{-}\frac{\pi}{N})=\rho(\textstyle\alpha^{-}\frac{\pi}{N})v_{N}
,
\displaystyle\textstyle v_{N}:=\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}{\Psi_{u_{0},N}(\alpha^{-}\frac{\pi}{N})}\right)\textstyle(1+\frac{1}{N}R_{N}(\alpha^{-}\frac{\pi}{N}))
, where
\displaystyle(v_{N})
is a bounded sequence, bounded from below by a constant
\displaystyle>0
. Then, an immediate adaptation of the proof of Proposition
5.3
(ii) implies that (
144
) remains valid with
\displaystyle\eta_{2}:=s_{N}^{-}
and
\displaystyle\eta_{1}:=s_{N}^{\prime}
, by multiplying
\displaystyle\rho(i\eta_{2})=\rho(is_{N}^{-})
with
\displaystyle v_{N}
.

The result is as follows. We have
\displaystyle{\rm Cl}_{2}^{\prime}(s_{N}^{-})=-{\rm Log}|2\sin(\alpha^{-}\pi N/2)|\sim_{N\to\infty}{\rm Log}(N)
, and hence
\displaystyle\textstyle o_{n\to\infty}\left(1/n|f^{\prime}(z_{0})|\right)=o_{n\to\infty}\left(1/N{\rm Log}(N)\right)
in the asymptotic formula of Proposition
5.3
(ii). Also
\displaystyle{\rm Cl}_{2}(s_{N}^{-})\sim_{N\to+\infty}-s_{N}^{-}{\rm Log}(s_{N}^{-})
,
\displaystyle e^{-\frac{N}{2i\pi}(s_{N}^{-})^{2}}\sim_{N\to+\infty}1
, and

\frac{\rho(is_{N}^{-})}{-i{\rm Log}|2\sin(s_{N}^{-}/2)|}\sim_{N\to+\infty}\frac{1}{i}\cdot e^{-\frac{{\bf l}_{1}^{\prime}\alpha^{-}}{2N}}\left(\frac{Ni}{\alpha^{-}\pi}\right)^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\rm Log}(\frac{N}{\alpha^{-}\pi})}.

Therefore, taking the bounded sequence
\displaystyle(v_{N})
into account as explained above, from (
144
) we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{[is_{N}^{\prime},is_{N}^{-}]}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{g_{N}(z)}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}dz

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sim_{N\to+\infty}i^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{3}{2}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}(\alpha^{-}\frac{\pi}{N}\cdot{\rm Log}(\frac{N}{\alpha^{-}\pi}))}e^{-\frac{{\bf l}_{1}^{\prime}\alpha^{-}}{2N}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{-}\pi}\right)^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{1}{2}}v_{N}\cdot\frac{1}{{\rm Log}(\frac{N}{\alpha^{-}\pi})}\cdot\dfrac{1}{N}

(145)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sim_{N\to+\infty}\dfrac{i^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{3}{2}}}{N^{1-\frac{\alpha^{-}}{2}}{\rm Log}(N)}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{-}\pi}\right)^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{-}}{2}}v_{N}.

We can now conclude. Consider the integral over
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+-}
on the RHS of (
143
). As in (
142
) one finds

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\int_{C_{a}^{+-}}g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi)}dz\right|=O_{N\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{2}}}\right).

Since
\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha^{-}
, it is a
\displaystyle o_{N\to+\infty}
of (
145
). From this and (
140
) we deduce

(146)

\int_{C_{a}^{+}}g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi)}dz\\
\sim_{N\to+\infty}i^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\dfrac{N^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{-}}{2}}}{{\rm Log}(N)}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{-}\pi}\right)^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{-}}{2}}v_{N}.

This concludes Step 3.

Step 4: Deformed contour in case (a), integral
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(i\pi)
.

Similarly, in the situation of Figure
3
we can deform
\displaystyle C_{N}^{\pm}
to the contour shown in the figure except in the case
\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}({\rm Log}(u_{0}))>0
, where the contour must circumvent
\displaystyle L_{1}
in a small subarc close to
\displaystyle 0
and joining a point
\displaystyle-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
,
\displaystyle\upsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
, to
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
, if
\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}({\rm Log}(u_{0}))<0
, or the contour must circumvent
\displaystyle L_{2}
in a small subarc close to
\displaystyle 2i\pi
and joining
\displaystyle-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}+2i\pi
to
\displaystyle 2i\pi-is_{N}^{+}
, if
\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}({\rm Log}(u_{0}))>0
. Indeed, we can take such subarcs to be
\displaystyle C^{+}_{a}
in the first case, and
\displaystyle\bar{C}^{+}_{a}+2i\pi
in the second case, with
\displaystyle C^{+}_{a}
defined as in Step 3, and
\displaystyle\bar{C}^{+}_{a}
the complex conjugate of
\displaystyle C^{+}_{a}
but with endpoint
\displaystyle-is_{N}^{+}
instead of
\displaystyle-is_{N}^{-}
.

Here we get the following horizontal derivative:

\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\Im(f_{+}(x+it))=2t-\arg\left(1-e^{x+it}\right)+\pi,

which is positive when
\displaystyle t>0
. Thus the integrals of
\displaystyle e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}\Im(f_{+}(z))}
over the subarcs
\displaystyle C^{+}_{a}
or
\displaystyle\bar{C}^{+}_{a}+2i\pi
will be dominated by a constant times the same integrals over the projections of these sub-arcs on the vertical axis, where we know that
\displaystyle\Im(f_{+}(z))={\rm Cl}_{2}(t)
. By the same arguments as in Step 3 for
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
one proves that
\displaystyle\textstyle\max_{\bar{C}^{+}_{a}+2i\pi}\left(\frac{|g_{N}(z)|}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}\right)<+\infty
. Then, the same reasoning as from (
140
) to the inequality in (
142
) gives

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{C}^{+}_{a}+2i\pi}g_{N}(z)\right.

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left.e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,i\pi)}dz\right|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}\max_{\bar{C}^{+}_{a}+2i\pi}\left(\frac{|g_{N}(z)|}{N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}}\right)\cdot O_{N\to\infty}(1)\cdot\int_{2\pi-s_{N}}^{2\pi}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}{\rm Cl}_{2}(t)}dt

(147)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad=N^{({\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}+i\pi)/2i\pi}O_{N\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

where the asymptotics of the last integral were obtained in Section
4.3.1
. On the other hand, as in (
146
) we have:

(148)

\int_{C_{a}^{+}}g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,i\pi)}dz\sim_{N\to+\infty}i^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\dfrac{N^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{-}}{2}}}{{\rm Log}(N)}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{-}\pi}\right)^{\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{-}}{2}}v_{N}.

For future reference in Step 6, we note that (
147
) is negligible with respect to (
148
), since
\displaystyle\alpha^{-}>0
and
\displaystyle(v_{N})
is bounded from below by a constant
\displaystyle>0
, and (
148
) tends to
\displaystyle+\infty
as
\displaystyle N\to+\infty
, since
\displaystyle\textstyle\mathfrak{R}(\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}-\frac{1}{2})>0
by our choice
\displaystyle a_{0}\geq 2
.

Step 5: Checking the assumptions of Proposition
5.4
away from the ends
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
,
\displaystyle is_{N}^{+}
.

By Steps 2 to 4 the function
\displaystyle g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{\pm}(z,-2i\pi,\ell)}
is holomorphic on a domain where we can deform the contour
\displaystyle C_{N}^{-}
or
\displaystyle C_{N}^{+}
to those described in Step 2 (Figure
7
or
\displaystyle[is_{N}^{-},is_{N}^{+}]
), Step 3 (Figure
8
), and Step 4. By Cauchy’s theorem the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,\pm}(\ell)
is unchanged if one replaces its contour by any of these ones.

By following the analysis of Sections
4.4
–
4.5
we now check that the hypothesis of Proposition
5.4
(ii) are satisfied by the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,\pm}(\ell)
, restricted to subcontours that stay away from the ends
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
and
\displaystyle is_{N}^{+}
in the situations of Figures
7
and
8
, and by the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,-}(i\pi)
on a subcontour of
\displaystyle[is_{N}^{-},is_{N}^{+}]
. Namely, these subcontours go from
\displaystyle 2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}
to
\displaystyle-i\eta
in Figure
8
, and from
\displaystyle i\eta
to
\displaystyle 2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}
in Figure
7
, where
\displaystyle\eta>s_{N}^{-}
,
\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}>s_{N}^{+}
, and one deformes slightly the contour near
\displaystyle s_{N}^{-}
and
\displaystyle 2i\pi-is_{N}^{+}
so that it goes first vertically to
\displaystyle i\eta
and finishes vertically from
\displaystyle 2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}
to
\displaystyle 2i\pi-is_{N}^{+}
, respectively. The subcontour goes from
\displaystyle i\eta
to
\displaystyle 2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}
vertically in the situation of the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,-}(i\pi)
.

Recall that the critical point
\displaystyle z_{0}
of
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\pm}(z,-2i\pi,\ell)
, where
\displaystyle\ell=i\pi
or
\displaystyle 2i\pi
, is:
\displaystyle\textstyle z_{0}=i\frac{\pi}{3}
for
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{-}(z;-2i\pi,i\pi)
,
\displaystyle\textstyle z_{0}={\rm Log}((\sqrt{5}-1)/2)
for
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{+}(z;-2i\pi,2i\pi)
, and
\displaystyle\textstyle z_{0}={\rm Log}((\sqrt{5}+1)/2+i\pi
for
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{-}(z;-2i\pi,2i\pi)
.

In each case
\displaystyle g_{N}(z)e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}\mathcal{L}_{\pm}(z,-2i\pi,\ell)}
is holomorphic in a neighborhood of
\displaystyle z_{0}
, and by the formulas (
120
) and (
135
) we see immediately that it is non vanishing at
\displaystyle z=z_{0}
, since
\displaystyle\rho(z_{0})\neq 0
. In Lemma
3.5
we proved that
\displaystyle\textstyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}\Psi_{u_{0},N}(z_{0})\right)
converges uniformly to
\displaystyle 1
on compact neighborhoods of
\displaystyle z_{0}
in
\displaystyle U
, and from (
130
) and the defining formula of
\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{u_{0},N}(z)
one finds easily an upper bound for
\displaystyle|R_{N}(z)|
, independent of
\displaystyle N
, in a sufficiently small compact neighborhood of
\displaystyle\textstyle z_{0}
. Hence the hypothesis of Proposition
5.4
(ii) are satisfied in each case.

Step 6: Comparing the asymptotics on subcontours, and conclusion.

Here we combine the Steps
\displaystyle 1
to
\displaystyle 5
to describe asymptotics of the integrals
\displaystyle I_{N,\pm}(\ell)
.

Consider the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(i\pi)
. The analysis of Section
4.4.2
can be repeated verbatim for the integral restricted to the subcontour from
\displaystyle 2i\pi-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
to
\displaystyle-\upsilon^{\prime\prime}
in Step 4 (Figure
3
). This analysis shows again that this integral is a
\displaystyle O_{N\to+\infty}(1)
summand of
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(i\pi)
. By Step 4 the integrals (
147
) and (
148
), along the arcs
\displaystyle\bar{C}^{+}_{a}+2i\pi
and
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
, have distinct growths, and (
148
) dominates (
147
) and is not bounded. Then the integral (
148
) dominates the asymptotics of
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(i\pi)
, which are therefore of the form stated in Lemma
5.5
.

Consider the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,-}(i\pi)
. By Step 5 and Proposition
5.4
(ii) the analysis of Section
4.4.2
can be repeated verbatim for the integral restricted to the subcontour
\displaystyle[i\eta,2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}]
. This analysis shows again that this integral grows exponentially, with growth rate equal to
\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Vol}(M)
. The integrals on the subcontours
\displaystyle[is_{N}^{-},i\eta]
and
\displaystyle[2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime},2i\pi-is_{N}^{+}]
have polynomial or subpolynomial growth, like in (
147
) and (
148
), so the asymptotics of
\displaystyle I_{N,-}(i\pi)
are of the form stated in Lemma
5.5
.

A similar reasoning applies to the integrals
\displaystyle I_{N,-}(2i\pi)
and
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(2i\pi)
by using the asymptotics computed in Step 3 for these integrals restricted to the small subcontours near
\displaystyle 0
or
\displaystyle 2i\pi
, and the asymptotics computed from Step 5 for the integrals restricted to the subcontour from
\displaystyle i\eta
to
\displaystyle 2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}
in Figure
7
, and to the subcontour from
\displaystyle 2i\pi-i\eta^{\prime}
to
\displaystyle-i\eta
in Figure
8
. These asymptotics from Step 5 have equivalents of order
\displaystyle 1/\sqrt{N}
, as in (
115
). The integral
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(2i\pi)
restricted to the small subcontour
\displaystyle C_{a}^{+}
has the asymptotics (
146
), and the integral
\displaystyle I_{N,-}(2i\pi)
restricted to the small subcontour
\displaystyle[is_{N}^{-},i\eta]
has the asymptotics (
146
) multiplied with
\displaystyle-e^{-\alpha^{-}\pi}
(the sign is because the contour starts at
\displaystyle is_{N}^{-}
, and the factor
\displaystyle e^{-\alpha^{-}\pi}
comes for the change of
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{+}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi)
to
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{-}(z,-2i\pi,2i\pi)
).
Since
\displaystyle a_{0}\geq 4
, we have
\displaystyle\textstyle\mathfrak{R}(\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}-\frac{1}{2})>-\frac{1}{2}
, and therefore these small integrals dominate the asymptotics of
\displaystyle I_{N,+}(2i\pi)
and
\displaystyle I_{N,-}(2i\pi)
, respectively. This concludes the proof.
\displaystyle\Box

Recall Lemma
3.12
: the two cases (a) and (b) correspond to
\displaystyle{\bf l}_{1,\infty}^{\bf x}=i\pi
,
\displaystyle{\bf l}_{0,\infty}^{\bf x}=-2i\pi
and
\displaystyle{\bf l}_{1,\infty}^{\bf x}=2i\pi
,
\displaystyle{\bf l}_{0,\infty}^{\bf x}=-2i\pi
respectively, for both
\displaystyle{\bf x}={\bf u}
and
\displaystyle{\bf v}^{*}
.

Corollary 5.7
.

We have

\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\dfrac{2\pi}{N}{\rm Log}\left|\int_{C_{N}}e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}(z^{2}-{\bf l}_{1,N}^{\bf x}z)}\hat{S}_{N}({\bf l}_{0,N}^{\bf x}+z)\textstyle\coth(\frac{Nz}{2})dz\right|=\left\{\begin{matrix}\dfrac{1}{2}{\rm Vol}(M)&\text{in\ case}\ (a),\\
0&\text{in\ case}\ (b),\end{matrix}\right.

and

\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\dfrac{2\pi}{N}{\rm Log}\left|\Sigma_{N}({\bf x}_{0,N},{\bf x}_{1,N})\right|=\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\dfrac{2\pi}{N}{\rm Log}\left|\int_{C_{N}}e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}(z^{2}-{\bf l}_{1,N}^{\bf x}z)}\hat{S}_{N}({\bf l}_{0,N}^{\bf x}+z)\textstyle\coth(\frac{Nz}{2})dz\right|

for
\displaystyle{\bf x}={\bf u}
or
\displaystyle{\bf v}^{*}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.1
and the first two asymptotics in Lemma
5.5
, if
\displaystyle\ell=i\pi
we have
\displaystyle\textstyle\lim_{N\to+\infty}\textstyle\frac{2\pi}{N}{\rm Log}|2I_{N,-}(\ell)-2u_{0}I_{N,+}(\ell)|=\textstyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Vol}(M)
. When
\displaystyle\ell=2i\pi
, we can always choose
\displaystyle\alpha_{-}
so that
\displaystyle|u_{0}|e^{-\alpha^{-}\pi}\neq 1
. Then the two terms in the Log have different growths, and Lemma
5.5
implies
\displaystyle\textstyle\lim_{N\to+\infty}\textstyle\frac{2\pi}{N}{\rm Log}|2I_{N,-}(2i\pi)-2u_{0}I_{N,+}(2i\pi)|=0
. The first claim follows.

Consider the second claim. By Lemma
1.3
the limit on the right does not change if we multiply the integral by
\displaystyle N/4i\pi\hat{S}_{N}({\bf l}_{0,N}^{\bf x})
, since it is in
\displaystyle PM(N)
by Lemma
3.8
, using that
\displaystyle{\bf l}_{k,N}^{\bf x}\in(\pi i)\mathbb{Z}+O(N^{-1})
by (
84
) and (
89
)-(
90
). And it does not change either if we add
\displaystyle 1
to the term inside
\displaystyle{\rm Log}|.|
, again because of its asymptotics given by Lemma
5.5
.
\displaystyle\Box

Remark 5.8
.

The proof of Lemma
5.5
gives more than the stated limits or equivalents, and expliciting the phase factor
\displaystyle e^{i\theta}
in Proposition
5.4
and improving the asymptotics of Lemma
3.8
, one can deduce subexponential terms of
\displaystyle\Sigma_{N}({\bf x}_{0,N},{\bf x}_{1,N})
(see Lemma
3.11
), and therefore of
\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{red}(M,\rho,\kappa;\mathfrak{s})
by using the formula (
82
).

Indeed, consider for instance the case
\displaystyle\ell=i\pi
. In this case
\displaystyle f_{-}(z)={\rm Li}_{2}(e^{z})+z^{2}-i\pi z
, and the computations in Section
4.4.1
and the formula
\displaystyle\textstyle\mathfrak{R}({\rm Li}_{2}(e^{it}))=\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\cos(nt)}{n^{2}}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}-\frac{\pi t}{2}+\frac{t^{2}}{4}
(
[
63
]
) imply that
\displaystyle f_{-}
has the critical point
\displaystyle\textstyle z_{0}=\frac{i\pi}{3}
, and
\displaystyle\textstyle f_{-}(\frac{i\pi}{3})=\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}+i\frac{1}{2}{\rm Vol(M)}
,
\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}_{-}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}})=1+e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}}
. Also, from (
120
) we get
\displaystyle\textstyle g(e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}}):=\rho(e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}})=e^{-\frac{{\bf l}_{1}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}\cdot\frac{i\pi}{3}}(1-e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}})^{-\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{2i\pi}-\frac{1}{2}}=e^{-\frac{{\bf l}_{1}^{\prime}}{6}-\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{3}-\frac{i\pi}{3}}
, where we recall that
\displaystyle{\bf l}_{k}^{\prime}={\rm Log}(u_{k})+i\pi a_{k}
, for
\displaystyle x=u
or
\displaystyle v^{*}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{C_{N}}e^{\frac{N}{2i\pi}(z^{2}-{\bf l}_{1,N}^{\bf x}z)}\hat{S}_{N}({\bf l}_{0,N}^{\bf x}+z)\textstyle

\displaystyle\displaystyle\coth(\frac{Nz}{2})dz

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sim_{N\to+\infty}2I_{N,-}(i\pi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sim_{N\to+\infty}2\cdot\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{N}}e^{i\theta}\cdot e^{-\frac{{\bf l}_{1}^{\prime}}{6}-\frac{{\bf l}_{0}^{\prime}}{3}-\frac{i\pi}{3}}\cdot\frac{1}{3^{\frac{1}{4}}}e^{\frac{N}{2\pi}(\frac{1}{2}{\rm Vol}(M)-i\frac{\pi^{2}}{4})}

where
\displaystyle\theta:=\pi-\textstyle\frac{1}{2}\arg(-\frac{1}{f^{\prime\prime}_{-}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}})})
(see e.g.
[
55
, equation (19)]
).

Appendix A
The QHI of
\displaystyle S^{3}\setminus K
vs. the Kashaev invariant
\displaystyle\langle K\rangle_{N}

The Kashaev invariant of the figure-eight knot
\displaystyle K
is (
[
40
]
)

\langle K\rangle_{N}=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{j}|1-\zeta^{k}|^{2}}.

It coincides with the evaluation of
\displaystyle J_{N}(K)/J_{N}(U)
at
\displaystyle q=e^{\frac{2\pi i}{N}}
, where
\displaystyle J_{N}(K)
is the
\displaystyle N
-colored Jones polynomial of
\displaystyle K
and
\displaystyle U
the unknot (
[
45
]
). On another hand (see Section
3.4
)

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{red}(S^{3}\setminus K,\rho,\kappa;\mathfrak{s})

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\frac{[{\bf v}_{0}]_{N}g_{N}({\bf u}_{0})}{g_{N}({\bf v}_{0})}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=0}^{N-1}\zeta^{\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2}}\frac{\omega_{N}({\bf u}_{0},{\bf u}_{1}^{-1}|\beta)}{\omega_{N}({\bf v}_{0}/\zeta,{\bf v}_{1}^{-1}|\alpha)}.

Recall the notations in Section
2.2.2
, and in particular that
\displaystyle\kappa=\kappa^{X}\circ\widetilde{hol}
. We mentioned after (
80
) that
\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{red}(S^{3}\setminus K,\rho,\kappa;\mathfrak{s})
defines a rational function on
\displaystyle{}_{N}X_{hyp}(M)
. We can take
\displaystyle\kappa^{X}(\lambda_{K})
as a local coordinate on the smooth locus of
\displaystyle{}_{N}X_{hyp}(M)
. Then, the next formula shows that
\displaystyle\langle K\rangle_{N}
is a value of a function
\displaystyle J_{N}({\bf u}_{0},{\bf v}_{0}|0)
, which is the regular part of
\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{red}(S^{3}\setminus K,\rho,\kappa;\mathfrak{s})
in a neighborhood of
\displaystyle\kappa^{X}(\lambda_{K})=0
(which maps via
\displaystyle\pi_{N}^{X}
onto a neighborhood of
\displaystyle\delta_{\rho}(\lambda_{K})=0
in
\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{hyp}(M)
).

Lemma A.1
.

The rational functions
\displaystyle J_{N}(\cdot,\cdot|i)
,
\displaystyle i\in\{0,\ldots,N-1\}
, defined on
\displaystyle\mathbb{C}^{2}
by

J_{N}({\bf u}_{0},{\bf v}_{0}|i):=\frac{g_{N}({\bf u}_{0}){\bf v}_{0}^{2(N-1)}}{Ng_{N}({\bf v}_{0})}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\zeta^{4ij+i}\frac{\prod_{k=1}^{i+N-j-1}{\bf v}_{0}^{-2}(1-{\bf v}_{0}\zeta^{k})}{\prod_{k=1}^{i+j}{\bf u}_{0}^{-2}(1-{\bf u}_{0}\zeta^{k})},

are regular in a neighborhood of
\displaystyle({\bf u}_{0},{\bf v}_{0})=(1,1)
, and satisfy
\displaystyle J_{N}(1,1|0)=\langle K\rangle_{N}
and

(149)

{\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{red}(S^{3}\setminus K,\rho,\kappa;\mathfrak{s})=\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\kappa(\lambda_{K})^{-i}J_{N}({\bf u}_{0},{\bf v}_{0}|i).

Proof.

The first claim is clear, and the second comes as follows:

\displaystyle\displaystyle J_{N}(1,1|0)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{\prod_{k=1}^{N-j-1}(1-\zeta^{k})}{\prod_{k=1}^{j}(1-\zeta^{k})}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{\prod_{k=1}^{N-j-1}(1-\zeta^{k})\prod_{k=1}^{j}(1-\zeta^{-k})}{\prod_{k=1}^{j}|1-\zeta^{k}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{j}|1-\zeta^{k}|^{2}}=\langle K\rangle_{N},

using
\displaystyle\textstyle\prod_{k=1}^{N-1}(1-\zeta^{k})=N
. Finally, in the above formula of
\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{red}({\bf u}_{0},{\bf u}_{1},{\bf v}_{0},{\bf v}_{1})
put
\displaystyle\beta=i+j
,
\displaystyle\alpha=i+N-j
. Then

{\mathcal{H}}_{N}^{red}({\bf u}_{0},{\bf u}_{1},{\bf v}_{0},{\bf v}_{1})=\frac{[{\bf v}_{0}]_{N}g_{N}({\bf u}_{0})}{g_{N}({\bf v}_{0})}\sum_{i,j=0}^{N-1}\zeta^{4ij}{\bf u}_{1}^{-(i+j)}{\bf v}_{1}^{i+N-j}\frac{\prod_{k=1}^{i+N-j}(1-{\bf v}_{0}\zeta^{k-1})}{\prod_{k=1}^{i+j}(1-{\bf u}_{0}\zeta^{k})}.

Let us collect “global” factors in the summands. By equations (
31
)-(
33
) the boundary weight
\displaystyle\kappa:=\kappa_{\bf w}\in H^{1}(\partial\bar{M};\mathbb{C}^{*})
associated to
\displaystyle{\bf w}:=({\bf u}_{0},{\bf u}_{1},{\bf v}_{0},{\bf v}_{1})
satisfies

{\bf u}_{0}^{4}{\bf u}_{1}^{2}=\zeta^{-1}\kappa(\lambda_{K})\ ,\ {\bf u}_{0}{\bf u}_{1}{\bf v}_{0}{\bf v}_{1}=\zeta^{-1}\kappa(\mu_{K})^{-1}.

Using the edge relation
\displaystyle{\bf u}_{0}^{2}{\bf u}_{1}{\bf v}_{0}^{2}{\bf v}_{1}=1
we can write

{\bf u}_{1}^{-(i+j)}{\bf v}_{1}^{i+N-j}=({\bf u}_{0}^{4}{\bf u}_{1}^{2})^{-i}{\bf u}_{0}^{2(i+j)}{\bf v}_{0}^{-2(i-j)}{\bf v}_{1}^{N}.

Note that
\displaystyle[{\bf v}_{0}]_{N}{\bf v}_{1}^{N}=1/N(1-{\bf v}_{0})
. Put

J_{N}({\bf u}_{0},{\bf v}_{0}|i)=\frac{g_{N}({\bf u}_{0})}{Ng_{N}({\bf v}_{0})}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\zeta^{4ij+i}{\bf u}_{0}^{2(i+j)}{\bf v}_{0}^{-2(i-j)}\frac{\prod_{k=1}^{i+N-j-1}(1-{\bf v}_{0}\zeta^{k})}{\prod_{k=1}^{i+j}(1-{\bf u}_{0}\zeta^{k})}.

Then we obtain the formula (
149
). This concludes the proof.
\displaystyle\Box
